Laud-damn

a misprint of 0. Edd. in Wint. 11, 1, 143, which has been productive of much misinterpretation and many improbable conjectures. Accord- ing to some , it means to banish from the land ; to others, to stop one's urine; to others still, to set breastdeep in the earth and thus cause to die of hunger. Hanmer conjectured land-damm. Farmer laudanum, Collier lamback etc. Perhaps we ought to read: icould I knew the villain, I would — Lord damn him!
